The definition is also documented on the wire type `ReadFileResult.truncated` in remote/src/rpc-types.ts. Tests: the existing offset-past-end expectation was corrected to true, and a new test covers an offset-only tail read (true), a window exactly covering the file and a window overshooting it (both false), and an offset past the end of an empty file (false).

**Why it matters.** The two C++ consumers use the flag as their only signal that the content is partial: src/viewers/InternalUrlSchemeHandler.cpp and src/viewers/ViewerModel.cpp refuse to render partial documents, and src/editor/EditorController.cpp marks the buffer read-only so a save cannot overwrite the file with a fragment. Both currently pass offset 0 plus a length, so no shipped call path was affected; any future partial read would have been told the fragment was the whole file, and saving it back would have destroyed the rest of the file. Behaviour for offset 0 reads is unchanged.


#### X15 — Agent-status hook could emit structurally valid events with empty workspace and terminal identifiers

- **Kind:** Active bug
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `remote/src/hooks/oh-my-pi-hook.ts`, `remote/src/events.ts`, `remote/src/bridge.ts`, `remote/test/events.test.ts`, `remote/test/agent-hook.test.ts`, `remote/test/adapters.test.ts`

**What was wrong.** The installable hook script remote/src/hooks/oh-my-pi-hook.ts read its Dev Session and terminal identifiers from environment variables and defaulted both to the empty string. remote/src/events.ts validateEvent() only checked `typeof === "string"`, which an empty string satisfies, so a misconfigured hook produced a fully valid event that was relayed to the desktop client and filed under an identifier no sidebar row can match. Verified both halves in the source. Additionally found (and fixed) a third hole the report did not mention: remote/src/bridge.ts builds its outgoing events with makeEvent() and never calls validateEvent(), so a stricter validateEvent alone would not have stopped the relay.

**Why it was wrong.** Two separate lenient checks. The hook chose 'never fail' over 'never emit nonsense' and substituted empty strings for missing configuration; the validator checked the type of the identifier fields but not that they name anything.

**What was done.** 1) remote/src/events.ts: added an exported type guard isEventIdentifier(value) that requires a string with at least one non-whitespace character, and validateEvent() now uses it for devSessionId and terminalId, so empty and whitespace-only identifiers are rejected (and therefore also rejected by parseEventLine). 2) remote/src/bridge.ts: processBridgeLine() now drops a socket line whose identifiers are blank, since it is the only gate on the socket-to-stdout relay path. 3) remote/src/hooks/oh-my-pi-hook.ts: added exported missingCoordinates(input), which names the unset or blank environment variables, and main() now writes one clear stderr line ('oh-my-pi-hook: not emitting <event>: OMP_DEV_SESSION_ID and OMP_TERMINAL_ID unset or blank ...') plus the usage reminder and returns normally without contacting the socket. It never throws and never exits non-zero, preserving the rule that a broken producer must not break the agent run. Tests added: blank/whitespace identifier rejection in validateEvent and parseEventLine (remote/test/events.test.ts); blank-identifier drop in processBridgeLine (remote/test/adapters.test.ts); and a hook test that listens on a real socket, asserts the refusal message, asserts that only the offending variable is named, and asserts the hook never opened a connection (remote/test/agent-hook.test.ts).

**Why it matters.** A misconfigured hook installation can no longer produce agent-status events attributed to nothing. Previously such an event reached the client, was stored under an empty workspace identifier where nothing could display it, and for the 'waiting for input' and 'finished with unseen output' states raised a desktop notification whose body was essentially a bare slash. The misconfiguration is now reported in the shell where it can be fixed. Well-formed events are unaffected; no existing test used an empty identifier.


#### X16 — Comments pointed at a constant defined in another file; the two schema-version numbers were not tied together

- **Kind:** Inconsistency
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `remote/src/rpc-types.ts`, `remote/test/rpc-mirror.test.ts`

**What was wrong.** Three comments in remote/src/rpc-types.ts instructed the reader to bump RPC_SCHEMA_VERSION, which is not declared in that file but in remote/src/codeharbord.ts. Separately, the C++ client declares its own compatibility floor kMinimumServerSchemaVersion in src/app/AppController.h. Both numbers are currently 4.

**Why it was wrong.** The comments were written from the point of view of the whole contract rather than of the file, and the cross-language check that existed only asserted the weaker relation.

**What was done.** The three comments now say the constant is defined in remote/src/codeharbord.ts and not in that file. In remote/test/rpc-mirror.test.ts the existing check (which already read the C++ header, and already failed loudly with an explicit assertion when the constant could not be parsed) was tightened from `RPC_SCHEMA_VERSION >= client minimum` to strict equality, with a comment explaining both failure directions: a floor above the server breaks every connection at the compatibility gate, and a server above the floor lets the client adopt a contract it was never taught. The 'must parse' assertion was kept and its message now includes the resolved header path.

**Why it matters.** A future bump on either side without the matching bump on the other now fails remote/test/rpc-mirror.test.ts instead of surfacing as a runtime handshake failure or a missing method against a live server. Both values are 4 today, so the tightened assertion passes as the tree stands.


#### X17 — Request dispatcher concurrency and ordering were undocumented; in-flight count deliberately left unbounded

- **Kind:** Footgun
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `remote/src/codeharbord.ts`

**What was wrong.** runStdio() in remote/src/codeharbord.ts starts each request line's handler without awaiting the previous one, so responses are written in completion order rather than arrival order, and nothing limits how many handlers run at once. Confirmed by reading the function.

**Why it was wrong.** The dispatch loop was written for throughput and the resulting guarantees (and non-guarantees) were never stated anywhere the next reader would look.

**What was done.** Added a comment block above the line handler stating plainly that responses may be written out of order and that the client must match a response to its call by the `id` field alone, never by position, and naming the C++ counterpart that does exactly that (ch::CodeharbordClient in src/remote/CodeharbordClient.cpp keys pending callbacks in a QHash<int, ResponseCallback> by request id, verified by reading that file). I deliberately did NOT add a concurrency bound, and the comment records why: a real bound needs real backpressure, and both available forms are more dangerous than the current behaviour. Pausing standard input also stalls the watch-notification stream and any request that would let a stalled client progress, because everything shares one SSH channel; a queue that drops a line leaves the client waiting forever for a reply. Meanwhile the exposure is small: the only producer is our own client, whose pending calls are bounded by user actions; no handler waits on another request, so nothing can deadlock; per-request memory is bounded by the client's own read cap; and the one ordering hazard that would be a genuine bug, two concurrent writes to the same path racing their revision check against their rename, is already serialized per path by the write locks in remote/src/files.ts.

**Why it matters.** No behaviour change. The next reader now knows the ordering contract instead of having to infer it, and the decision not to bound is recorded with its reasoning so it can be revisited deliberately rather than by accident.


#### X18 — Missing lookup indexes added without a schema-version bump, and applied on every open

- **Kind:** Footgun
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `remote/sql/indexes.sql`, `remote/sql/schema.sql`, `remote/src/workspace.ts`, `remote/test/workspace.test.ts`

**What was wrong.** The four lookup columns groups.server_id, dev_sessions.group_id, viewer_panes.dev_session_id and terminal_panes.dev_session_id had no index, so every listing scanned the whole table. The reported obstacle is real and I confirmed it: migrate() in remote/src/workspace.ts applies the schema definition only when the stored schema version is BELOW the code's target, so DDL added to remote/sql/schema.sql alone never reaches a database that is already at the current version.

**Why it was wrong.** Indexes were treated as part of the versioned schema definition, and that definition is only re-applied during a version upgrade. Raising the version to force it is not available in this slice: WORKSPACE_SCHEMA_VERSION is mirrored by WorkspaceDb::kSchemaVersion in src/persistence/WorkspaceDb.h, and two tests pin all three copies together (remote/test/schema.test.ts on the SQL seed literal, and src/persistence/tests/tst_workspacedb.cpp, which parses both remote/sql/schema.sql and remote/src/workspace.ts and compares them against the C++ constant). I am not allowed to edit either C++ file, so a bump here would have red-lit the C++ suite.

**What was done.** DECLINED the version bump; added the indexes in a way that needs none. New file remote/sql/indexes.sql holds four `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` statements (idx_groups_server_id, idx_dev_sessions_group_id, idx_viewer_panes_dev_session_id, idx_terminal_panes_dev_session_id) with a header explaining why they are not in schema.sql. openWorkspace() reads that file at module load, next to the existing schema read, and executes it on EVERY open, immediately after migrate() so the tables already exist. The statements are idempotent and hold no data, so a repeat open costs four no-op statements. schema.sql gained a header note pointing at the new file. Two tests in remote/test/workspace.test.ts: one creates a database, asserts the four indexes exist, seeds rows, drops the indexes while leaving the stored version at the current value (exactly the case the migration runner cannot fix), reopens and asserts the indexes are back, the rows are untouched and the version is unchanged, then opens a third time to show it is a no-op; the other asks SQLite's own query planner (EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN) whether the session-by-group query actually chooses idx_dev_sessions_group_id, so a wrong column name fails instead of passing as 'the index exists'.

**Why it matters.** No wire-visible change: the reported schema version in server.info is untouched, so the client's compatibility gate (kMinimumServerSchemaVersion in src/app/AppController.h) still passes and no C++ change is required. Existing databases gain the indexes the next time codeharbord opens them. Listings of a server's groups, a group's sessions and a session's panes become index lookups instead of table scans. The release artifact already packages the whole remote/sql directory (.github/workflows/release.yml tars `dist package.json sql`), so the new file ships without a packaging change.


*Closing note:* Files written: remote/src/files.ts, remote/src/rpc-types.ts, remote/src/events.ts, remote/src/bridge.ts, remote/src/hooks/oh-my-pi-hook.ts, remote/src/codeharbord.ts, remote/src/workspace.ts, remote/sql/schema.sql, remote/sql/indexes.sql (new), remote/test/files.test.ts, remote/test/events.test.ts, remote/test/adapters.test.ts, remote/test/agent-hook.test.ts, remote/test/workspace.test.ts, remote/test/rpc-mirror.test.ts. Nothing outside remote/ was written; C++ and docs were read only. As instructed, no build or test command was run, so these changes are unexecuted: they were kept compile-safe by checking every touched declaration against its callers. Two specific compile risks I handled by construction rather than by running tsc: in remote/src/bridge.ts the pre-existing `typeof ... !== "string"` checks were kept alongside the new blank check, because they are what narrows the fields to `string` for the makeEvent call below; and the new casts in tests follow the `.all() as Array<{...}>` form already used in remote/test/schema.test.ts. One behavioural expectation in an existing test was intentionally changed as part of X14: remote/test/files.test.ts previously asserted truncated===false for a read past the end of a non-empty file, which is the bug, and now asserts true. Two mild residual risks worth the lead knowing: the new EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN test depends on SQLite choosing the index for an equality lookup on an empty table (it does, but it is a planner assertion), and the hook refusal test asserts the exact wording 'unset or blank' of the new stderr message.

### Browser bundles — 3 item(s)

#### X19 — Editor web page cleared its unsaved-changes mark even when the user typed during the save

- **Kind:** Active bug
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `src/web/editor/src/index.ts`

**What was wrong.** In mountEditor() in src/web/editor/src/index.ts, the handler for the C++ side's `saved` signal set the page-local `dirty` flag to false unconditionally. The C++ half (ch::EditorController::save() in src/editor/EditorController.cpp) keeps its own dirty flag set and publishes file state "Modified" when the buffer changed while the write was in flight, so the status label in the page and the state the C++ controller reports disagreed.

**Why it was wrong.** The page had no record of whether its Monaco model changed between issuing a save and receiving the success reply, so it treated every success as 'buffer now equals the file'. The C++ half does track this, but only via its own edit counter, which advances on the reportContent() slot - and the page debounces that slot by 500 ms, so a keystroke during a short save is often not yet known to C++ either.

**What was done.** Added two page-local counters next to the existing `dirty` flag: `editSerial`, incremented on every user edit in editor.onDidChangeModelContent(), and `baselineEditSerial`, snapshotted in requestSave() (the bytes handed to the host) and in the contentLoaded handler (a host-driven load re-baselines the buffer against the file). The `saved` handler now computes `editedDuringSave = editSerial !== baselineEditSerial`, sets `dirty` to that value instead of false, and when it is true calls the existing flushReport() so the still-pending debounced reportContent() is delivered immediately. That flush is what makes the two halves agree: it marks the C++ controller dirty again and re-creates the crash-recovery snapshot that a successful save had just discarded. If the debounced report already went out during the write, no timer is pending, flushReport() does nothing, and C++ was already dirty.

**Why it matters.** The status label now matches the C++ controller's state after a save that raced a keystroke, and the edits typed during that save are guaranteed to be present in the server-side crash-recovery snapshot instead of only living in the page until the next debounce.


#### X20 — Claim that the editor page's teardown comment lies about flushing a pending snapshot

- **Kind:** Documentation error
- **Outcome:** Not fixed (explained)
- **Files:** `src/web/editor/src/index.ts`

**What was wrong.** The report says the comment above editor.onDidDispose reads 'Flush any pending snapshot and release Monaco when the surface goes away' while the body only clears the debounce timer.

**Why it was wrong.** The reported comment exists only in the committed version of the file (verified with `git show HEAD:src/web/editor/src/index.ts`, line 300). Wave one already rewrote that region: the comment now reads 'A host that disposes the editor directly (not through the returned host) must not leave a timer that would call getValue() on a dead editor.' above `editor.onDidDispose(cancelReport)`, which is an accurate description of cancel-only behaviour. The actual flush was moved to the correct place - EditorHost.dispose() calls flushReport() before editor.dispose() - and connectEditor() wires `window.addEventListener("pagehide", () => host.dispose())`, so page teardown and pane close both flush.

**What was done.** No change. Flushing inside onDidDispose is not possible anyway: that callback runs during editor.dispose(), when editor.getValue() would touch an already-disposed model. Pre-dispose flushing, which is what the code does, is the only correct placement.

**Why it matters.** None; the reported inconsistency no longer exists in the working tree.


#### X21 — Terminal page's cached 'last reported visibility' could swallow a correcting report after another writer moved the C++ flag

- **Kind:** Latent bug
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `src/web/terminal/src/index.ts`

**What was wrong.** ch::TerminalBridge::notifyViewVisible() (src/terminal/TerminalBridge.cpp) is called from three unrelated places: the terminal web page's own visibility detection, the QML pane's `onVisibleChanged` (src/qml/TerminalPaneView.qml line 139), and ch::TerminalBridge::ready(), which reports visible when the page mounts. The page kept a `reportedVisible` variable and skipped sending a value equal to it, but that variable only records what the PAGE last sent, not what ch::TerminalController currently holds - so once another writer moved the controller's flag, the page's cache was stale and suppressed exactly the report that would have corrected it.

**Why it was wrong.** Two layers wrote the same piece of controller state with no single owner, and the page deduplicated against its own local memory of the value rather than against the controller's real state.

**What was done.** Made the page's report authoritative and unconditional in src/web/terminal/src/index.ts: removed the `reportedVisible` cache, so reportVisibility() always sends the conjunction of IntersectionObserver intersection and document.visibilityState, and the dispose() path always sends false. ch::TerminalController::setViewVisible() already ignores a value equal to the one it holds, so a redundant report costs one WebChannel message and nothing else. Added a comment block documenting the ownership: the page is authoritative because it is the only layer that can observe in-document collapse (IntersectionObserver) as well as the whole page being un-rendered (document.visibilityState); the QML `onVisibleChanged` report and ready()'s report are strict coarser subsets that exist only because they can speak before a renderer exists.

**Why it matters.** The controller's visible/hidden flag now converges on the page's full computation after any event, so output can no longer be flushed at a renderer nobody can see (or retained while one is visible) because two writers disagreed.


*Closing note:* X21 conclusion for routing a QML-side change: no QML change is required for correctness, and I recommend leaving src/qml/TerminalPaneView.qml line 139 as it is. An invisible QML pane item is precisely what makes Qt WebEngine stop rendering the view, which is what turns document.visibilityState to "hidden" - so the QML report is a strict subset of what the page computes, and it is still useful because it can report a hidden pane during the window when no page/renderer is attached yet. If the lead wants symmetry, the only worthwhile QML-side edit is a one-line comment on onVisibleChanged saying it is the coarse pre-renderer signal and that src/web/terminal/src/index.ts is authoritative once mounted. A related third writer worth noting (C++ side, not mine to change): ch::TerminalBridge::ready() calls setViewVisible(true) unconditionally; that is now always corrected, because IntersectionObserver's initial callback fires after ready() and the page no longer suppresses an equal-looking value.

No build or test commands were run. Files written: src/web/terminal/src/index.ts, src/web/editor/src/index.ts only.

### Desktop client — QML user interface — 7 item(s)

#### X22 — QML module's CMake file duplicated the whole web-bundle block and ignored the npm install exit status

- **Kind:** Footgun
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `src/qml/CMakeLists.txt`

**What was wrong.** src/qml/CMakeLists.txt contained two nearly identical ~75-line blocks, one for the Monaco editor browser bundle (src/web/editor) and one for the xterm.js terminal bundle (src/web/terminal). Both did the same five things: glob the bundle's TypeScript sources, decide whether the built output is missing or older than those sources, find the `npm` program, run `npm install` and then `npm run build` for that workspace, and finally either embed the built files as Qt resources, print a warning (when -DCODEHARBOR_SKIP_WEB_BUNDLE=ON), or stop configuration with a hard error. They differed only in directory name, resource-set name, file list and wording. Separately, both blocks captured the exit status of `npm install` into a variable and then never looked at it, so a failed dependency install fell through to the bundle build step, which then failed with a confusing 'esbuild not found'-class error far from the real cause. Both claims were verified in the file before changing anything.

**Why it was wrong.** The terminal bundle support was added by copy-pasting the editor bundle block instead of parameterising it, and the `RESULT_VARIABLE _ch_npm_install_rc` of the install step was written but no `if(NOT ... EQUAL 0)` check was ever added for it (unlike the build step, which does check its own status).

**What was done.** Replaced both blocks with one CMake function, `codeharbor_embed_web_bundle`, taking keyword arguments DIR (directory under src/web, which is also the npm workspace name), RESOURCE (Qt resource-set name), LABEL and PANE (wording used in configure messages) and FILES (dist file names to embed). It is called twice, once per bundle. The staleness comparison, the `-DCODEHARBOR_SKIP_WEB_BUNDLE=ON` escape hatch, the CMAKE_CONFIGURE_DEPENDS registration and the identical three-way ending (embed / warn / fatal error) are preserved exactly. The `npm install` step now checks its exit status and, on failure, stops configuration with message(FATAL_ERROR) that includes the captured npm output and names the command to run by hand (`npm install`), plus the escape-hatch option. Install output is captured into a variable rather than discarded, so it is available for that error message but still silent on success. The two per-bundle calls declare the differing values, so there is now one place to fix.

**Why it matters.** A fix or hardening in the bundle logic can no longer land on one bundle and silently miss the other, and a broken dependency install now fails at the real cause with an actionable message instead of one step later with a misleading one. This is configure-time CMake; the lead's re-configure exercises it. Note one intentional behaviour change: a failed `npm install` is now fatal, where previously it could proceed and embed a stale-but-existing bundle.


#### X23 — Terminal pane's geometry-changed handler could re-enter attachNow() and open a second shell

- **Kind:** Latent bug
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `src/qml/TerminalPaneView.qml`

**What was wrong.** In src/qml/TerminalPaneView.qml the Connections block on the C++ bridge answers its geometryChanged signal by calling the pane's attachNow() function. attachNow()'s only protection against being called again while it is already running was `if (pane.attached) return`, and `pane.attached` is assigned from the RETURN value of the C++ call pane.factory.attach(...). It is therefore still false for the entire duration of that call, so anything the C++ side emitted synchronously from inside attach() and routed back to attachNow() would start a second attach and leak the first shell. Confirmed by reading the file; today it is safe only because the C++ bridge is documented to emit geometryChanged only for sizes reported by the web page.

**Why it was wrong.** The state flag used as the guard is derived from the result of the very call that needs guarding, so it cannot cover the window during that call.

**What was done.** Added a dedicated `property bool attaching: false` to the pane, checked first in attachNow() alongside the existing conditions, and set around only the pane.factory.attach(...) call using try/finally so an exception thrown out of the C++ call cannot leave the pane permanently unattachable (which would also disable its own Retry button). Comments explain why `attached` cannot serve as the guard and that this is the QML-side half of an invariant also documented on the C++ side. The C++ side was not touched.

**Why it matters.** The pane can no longer open two shells for one terminal, whatever a future change on either side of the bridge does. No observable behaviour changes in the normal path: `attaching` is false everywhere except inside the attach call.


#### X24 — Inline text viewer cannot cancel a superseded remote read

- **Kind:** Nit
- **Outcome:** Not fixed (explained)
- **Files:** `src/qml/ViewerTextView.qml`

**What was wrong.** reload() in src/qml/ViewerTextView.qml starts a remote file read whenever the pane's URL changes and never cancels a read already in flight for the previous URL. Late answers are filtered by comparing the path in the reply against the current URL, so wrong content is never shown, but the abandoned read still runs to completion and occupies the remote file service. I checked whether a cancel is possible: src/viewers/ViewerModel.h exposes only readTextFile(path) plus the textFileRead / textFileError signals and has no cancel entry point of any kind, so QML cannot cancel the read today.

**Why it was wrong.** The QML-facing file-reading facade (ch::ViewerModel) offers a fire-and-forget read with no request handle and no cancel method, so a caller has nothing to cancel with.

**What was done.** Cancellation was NOT implemented, because it cannot be done from QML. Instead reload() now carries a comment recording exactly that: the previous read is not cancelled, its answer is discarded by the path comparison in the Connections block, and cancelling properly requires a new method on ch::ViewerModel (a C++ file owned by another agent, which I did not edit). Nothing else in the view changed. The second half of the item — whether the new C++ error text 'file is too large to display inline' reads acceptably in this pane — was checked: the pane renders it through the status Label as 'Error: file is too large to display inline' in the pane's error colour, as plain text, centred over the empty buffer. That reads as a complete, understandable sentence, so the presentation was deliberately left alone.

**Why it matters.** Behaviour is unchanged; the limitation is now documented where a maintainer will see it. To actually fix the wasted connection time, ch::ViewerModel needs a cancel method (for example a cancelTextFile(path), or a request id returned by readTextFile and honoured when the reply arrives) — that is a C++ change in src/viewers/ViewerModel.h/.cpp and belongs to the owner of that slice.


#### X25 — Editor pane's header comment described a context property that does not exist

- **Kind:** Documentation error
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `src/qml/EditorPaneView.qml`

**What was wrong.** The WIRING comment block at the top of src/qml/EditorPaneView.qml claimed 'editorController — context property: the ch::EditorController instance'. Verified: no such context property exists. main.cpp installs `editorFactory` (a ch::EditorFactory), and the pane creates its own controller per pane from it. A reader following the comment would look for a shared controller that is deliberately not there.

**Why it was wrong.** The comment predates the change from one shared editor controller to one controller per pane, and was not updated with the code.

**What was done.** Rewrote that bullet to describe `editorFactory`: the ch::EditorFactory context property that mints THIS pane's own ch::EditorController (one per pane, so two panes never share a path and revision), and noted that there is deliberately no shared controller context property. The statement that the controller is exposed to JavaScript under the WebChannel object name 'editor' was kept, as it is correct.

**Why it matters.** The file's wiring documentation now matches the code, so a maintainer wiring a new host installs the right context property.


#### X26 — Editor pane created its C++ controller through an unguarded context-property lookup

- **Kind:** Footgun
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `src/qml/EditorPaneView.qml`

**What was wrong.** Confirmed the asymmetry with the terminal pane. src/qml/TerminalPaneView.qml resolves its injected factory defensively: `property var factory: (typeof terminalFactory !== "undefined") ? terminalFactory : null`, and then creates its controller only `if (factory)`. src/qml/EditorPaneView.qml instead did `property var controller: editorFactory.create(root)` — an unguarded lookup of an injected name. In QML an unknown name throws a ReferenceError, which aborts the binding pass building the pane rather than producing an inert pane.

**Why it was wrong.** The editor pane was written before the terminal pane's guard convention and never adopted it.

**What was done.** Added `property var factory: (typeof editorFactory !== "undefined") ? editorFactory : null` and changed the controller to `property var controller: root.factory ? root.factory.create(root) : null`, i.e. exactly the terminal pane's pattern. Every existing use of `root.controller` was already null-checked (start() and Component.onCompleted), so nothing else needed changing and the property name tests read (`controller`) is unchanged. For the pane's second injected object, `viewers`, the guard was deliberately NOT added and the reason is now recorded in the file: every viewer view in this module (ViewerImageView, ViewerPdfView, ViewerBinaryView) and the terminal pane itself call `viewers.internalProfile()` unguarded, so guarding it here alone would introduce a second convention; and nothing can instantiate this pane without `viewers` anyway, because the only creator of an EditorPaneView is ViewerPane, which has already called viewers.viewKind() to decide the file belongs here. I also confirmed I must not wrap the WebEngineView in a Loader the way the terminal pane does: the live editor test (src/editor/tests/tst_liveeditor.cpp) finds the view by scanning the pane's direct children for a runJavaScript function, and a Loader would hide it.

**Why it matters.** A host that forgets to install `editorFactory` now gets a pane with no controller instead of a thrown ReferenceError mid-construction, and the editor pane follows the same guard convention as its sibling terminal pane.


#### X27 — Nothing showed the user that an open editor file is disconnected or in conflict

- **Kind:** Active bug
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `src/qml/EditorPaneView.qml`

**What was wrong.** The file-state word published by ch::EditorController (Q_PROPERTY fileState, NOTIFY fileStateChanged) was consumed only by the browser page's status line, which prints the raw word. Two of those states matter a lot and had no visual treatment: 'disconnected' (the SSH transport is down; the controller stays there from onTransportClosed until a transport is bound again, so nothing can be read or saved) and 'conflict' (the file changed on the server since it was loaded, so the save was refused). The terminal pane already gives a dropped connection a distinct treatment. I verified the exact state strings in src/models/SessionState.cpp toString(FileState) ('disconnected', 'conflict') and the exact property/signal names in src/editor/EditorController.h, and confirmed the state IS reachable from QML.

**Why it was wrong.** The pane never bound to the controller's fileState property, so the C++ state machine's most user-visible outcomes existed only inside the web page's status text.

**What was done.** Bound the state in src/qml/EditorPaneView.qml as `readonly property string fileState: root.controller ? root.controller.fileState : ""` (the same shape TerminalPaneView uses for its bridge's connectionState, which is likewise a QString property with an argument-carrying notify signal, so the pattern is proven in this codebase). Derived `dropped` and `conflicted` from it and added a 22-pixel banner across the top of the pane — the same convention TerminalPaneView uses for a pane that is loaded but not live: red (#45222c) for the state that risks the user's edits (conflict), amber (#3a2f1e) for the one that merely suspends them (disconnected), amber text (#f9e2af) at 11px, with a whole-sentence explanation rather than the state word. `dropped` also requires a file to actually be open, because a freshly created controller starts in the Disconnected state and would otherwise advertise a failure that has not happened. This needed `import QtQuick.Controls.Basic` for Label, matching the terminal pane's imports.

**Why it matters.** A user whose connection drops, or whose save was refused because someone else changed the file, now sees an unmissable explanation in the pane instead of a quiet word in the page's status line. Every name used was checked against src/editor/EditorController.h and src/models/SessionState.cpp, and the file passes qmllint 6.10.2 with no new warnings.


#### X28 — The four modal dialogs were drawn in the control library's default light theme

- **Kind:** Inconsistency
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `src/qml/AppDialog.qml`, `src/qml/SessionRow.qml`, `src/qml/SessionsSidebar.qml`, `src/qml/ConnectSheet.qml`, `src/qml/CMakeLists.txt`

**What was wrong.** Confirmed: the four modal dialogs — rename session (SessionRow.qml), new group and new Dev Session (SessionsSidebar.qml) and SSH connection details (ConnectSheet.qml) — were plain QtQuick.Controls.Basic Dialogs with no colours set. Every other surface in src/qml paints itself explicitly in the application's dark scheme, so these opened as a light sheet with black text and grey buttons on top of a dark window, and the text fields inside them inherited light-on-light colours too.

**Why it was wrong.** The dialogs were never given colours, and the Basic control style's default palette is light. I read the installed Basic style implementations (Dialog.qml, DialogButtonBox.qml, Button.qml, TextField.qml, TextArea.qml, Label.qml, ScrollBar.qml) and confirmed every part of a dialog takes its colour from the palette: the sheet from palette.window, its border from palette.dark, the bold title Label from palette.windowText, the button box from palette.window, its buttons from palette.button / palette.buttonText / palette.mid, and the fields from palette.base / palette.text / palette.placeholderText / palette.highlight.

**What was done.** Added one new component, src/qml/AppDialog.qml: a Dialog that sets nothing but colours, using the same hex values the hand-painted surfaces already use (#1e1e2e surface, #11111b wells, #313244 controls, #45475a borders, #cdd6f4 text, #6c7086 dimmed text, #89b4fa focus accent). Registered it in src/qml/CMakeLists.txt QML_FILES and changed the four call sites from `Dialog {` to `AppDialog {` — one word each; modality, position, standard buttons, content and all handlers stay at the call site, so nothing is restructured. One shared component rather than the same colour block pasted four times, for the same reason X22 exists. Checked the consumers: tst_sidebar finds newSessionDialog by objectName and calls accept() (both unchanged); tst_serverprofiles loads ConnectSheet.qml straight from the source directory, and I verified with qmllint 6.10.2 that AppDialog resolves there through the implicit same-directory import even without an explicit import path, as well as inside the module.

**Why it matters.** The four dialogs and their fields and buttons now match the rest of the window instead of looking like a foreign light-theme window, and any future colour change to dialogs happens in one file. No behaviour, structure, objectName or signal changed.


*Closing note:* All seven routed items were verified against the files before acting. Six are fixed; X24 is declined with a reason (cancellation is impossible from QML because ch::ViewerModel exposes no cancel method — a C++ change would be needed, and the C++ error wording it also asked about reads correctly in the pane, so the presentation was left alone). I wrote only to files under src/qml/ (CMakeLists.txt, AppDialog.qml [new], EditorPaneView.qml, TerminalPaneView.qml, ViewerTextView.qml, SessionRow.qml, SessionsSidebar.qml, ConnectSheet.qml) and read C++ headers/tests without editing them. No build or test command was run. As verification for the QML edits I ran the static checker qmllint 6.10.2 (the project's own Qt version) over every QML file I touched: no errors, and the only warnings are the pre-existing 'unqualified access' notes about injected context objects (app, viewers, editorFactory) that the untouched files already produce. The CMake change is configure-time and could not be checked by running cmake, so it was reviewed line by line: the function's keyword parsing, the CMAKE_CURRENT_SOURCE_DIR / DIRECTORY-property semantics inside a function, the resource-set names, the staleness comparison and the three-way ending all match the code they replaced, and I grepped the whole tree to confirm nothing outside src/qml/CMakeLists.txt referenced the variables the old blocks defined. No new tests were added: the terminal re-entrancy guard and the editor banner are presentation and defensive plumbing that the existing gates already load, and adding a new test executable would require CMake changes I could not build or run to validate.

### Documentation — 8 item(s)

#### X29 — Specification section 6.6 named agent states that do not exist

- **Kind:** Documentation error
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `docs/SPEC.md`

**What was wrong.** Section 6.6 of docs/SPEC.md listed the coarse agent states available without a harness-specific adapter as "connected", "activity detected", "idle", "disconnected". Three of those four are not values the system ever produces or understands.

**Why it was wrong.** The specification text was written before the fallback detector existed and was never reconciled with it. The class FallbackActivityDetector in remote/src/adapters/fallback.ts returns exactly "starting" (no output observed yet), "running" (output within the idle threshold) and "idle" (quiet for longer than the threshold). The set of legal values is the array AGENT_STATES in remote/src/events.ts, which contains starting, running, waiting_input, idle_unseen, idle, error, stopped, unknown - and no "connected", "activity detected" or "disconnected".

**What was done.** Rewrote section 6.6 to list the three values the code actually returns, with the meaning of each, and to state that the fallback deliberately draws from the same vocabulary as section 6.4 rather than inventing values the desktop client cannot interpret. Added an explicit note that loss of the SSH channel is a transport condition surfaced by the client's own connection state, which is why there is no "disconnected" agent state. Named remote/src/adapters/fallback.ts and remote/src/events.ts as the sources. The closing requirement ("must not pretend to reliably distinguish...") was left untouched.

**Why it matters.** Documentation only. Anyone implementing a new harness adapter from the specification would previously have emitted state strings that the client rejects.


#### X30 — Specification section 10.3 did not document the JSON-RPC framing rules the service enforces

- **Kind:** Documentation error
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `docs/SPEC.md`

**What was wrong.** Section 10.3 ("RPC") consisted of one sentence: "Use newline-delimited JSON or framed JSON-RPC." The remote service makes a number of specific, deliberate protocol choices that were nowhere written down.

**Why it was wrong.** The section was a placeholder. Every rule below was read out of remote/src/codeharbord.ts and remote/src/rpc-types.ts: one request object per line (runStdio reads lines with node:readline); batch requests unsupported - a JSON array fails isRpcRequest() and gets a single Invalid Request -32600 (the comment on dispatch() says so explicitly, and remote/test/adapters.test.ts:229 asserts it); blank lines skipped by the /\S/ test in the line handler; parse failure answered with id null and code -32700 in handleLine(); structurally invalid request answered with id null and -32600; params must be an object or array or the call fails with -32602; an unknown method (-32601) is checked before params validity, so it outranks bad params; a request with no id member returns null, meaning no response line at all, in every outcome; and RPC_REVISION_MISMATCH = -32001 in remote/src/rpc-types.ts is the one application-level code.

**What was done.** Replaced the single sentence in section 10.3 with a bulleted list of the eight enforced rules, each naming the error code and the reasoning, and pointing at remote/src/codeharbord.ts and remote/src/rpc-types.ts. Also recorded two adjacent facts the code makes clear: an explicit "id": null is a legal id and is echoed back (it is not treated as a notification), and server-to-client watch events use the same id-less notification form. I deliberately did NOT claim every rule has a dedicated test - I checked remote/test/ and found tests for the batch case, the notification case, the invalid-params case and the revision-mismatch code, but none specifically for blank lines or for a parse error.

**Why it matters.** Documentation only, but it removes real ambiguity: the previous wording permitted "framed JSON-RPC" and batch requests, neither of which the service accepts.


#### X31 — Specification section 12.1 said a changed host key could be approved; the code refuses unconditionally

- **Kind:** Documentation error
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `docs/SPEC.md`

**What was wrong.** Section 12.1 required "refusal of changed host keys unless explicitly approved", implying the application offers the user a way to accept a host key that differs from the stored one. It does not.

**Why it was wrong.** SshConnectionPool::verifyHostKey() in src/ssh/SshConnectionPool.cpp switches on the known-hosts verdict. For Verdict::Mismatch it emits hostKeyMismatch, emits an error, and returns false - the comment on that line reads "hard refusal (SPEC 12.1)". The user-decision callback m_hostKeyCallback is invoked only in the Verdict::Unknown branch, i.e. for a host that is not in the store at all. So the code is stricter than the specification, deliberately, and is self-consistent with its own comments; only the specification disagreed.

**What was done.** Rewrote the bullet in section 12.1 to require unconditional refusal of a changed host key, noting that this covers the same key presented under a different algorithm too, naming SshConnectionPool::verifyHostKey() as the implementation, and stating that a user who genuinely needs to accept a new key for an already-known host must edit the known-hosts file deliberately outside the application. Added one sentence of rationale: an in-app approve button on this path is exactly what an interceptor needs.

**Why it matters.** Documentation only. The previous wording could have been read as a bug report against correct, security-relevant code, or worse, as a licence to add an approval prompt on the mismatch path.


#### X32 — Wrong specification section cited for the two-browser-profile design

- **Kind:** Documentation error
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`

**What was wrong.** A note in docs/DEVELOPMENT.md explaining why the Chromium --single-process flag must not be added ended with "the viewer stack creates two by design (SPEC 7.2)". Section 7.2 of docs/SPEC.md is "Arbitrary Websites"; the section that actually mandates two separate browser profiles is 7.3, "Browser Profiles".

**Why it was wrong.** An off-by-one citation. Section 7.2 states that external pages must use a separate security context; section 7.3 is the one that says to use separate Qt WebEngine profiles.

**What was done.** Changed the citation to "SPEC 7.3, \"Browser Profiles\"". I then checked every other specification-section citation in docs/DEVELOPMENT.md and README.md: the only other one is "the client keeps no local database (SPEC 11.2)" in the dependency table, and section 11.2 ("Local State") does say the client may store only connection profiles, geometry, preferences, credential references and browser cache, and must not store project files - so that citation is correct and was left alone. README.md contains no numbered section citations at all.

**Why it matters.** Documentation only.


#### X33 — Three specification requirements are unimplemented, plus crash recovery is never offered, and nothing recorded it

- **Kind:** Documentation error
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `docs/SPEC.md`, `docs/PLAN.md`

**What was wrong.** Four gaps between the specification and the shipped product were undocumented, so the specification read as a description of working features. All four were verified in the code.

**Why it was wrong.** (a) Section 4.2 requires each sidebar row to show the number of active terminals, the number needing attention, an unsaved-file indicator and an error indicator. The enum SessionsModel::Roles in src/models/SessionsModel.h contains only NameRole, SubtitleRole, RowStateRole, IsGroupRole, CollapsedRole, IdRole and GroupIdRole - no counter roles and no file role, so the QML delegate has nothing to bind to. The error case IS covered, as one value of the aggregate state. (b) Section 8.2 requires unsaved-file state in both the pane header and the sidebar row. aggregateRowState() in src/models/SessionState.cpp takes five booleans - error, waiting-for-input, running, finished-unseen, connected - all sourced from terminal and coding-agent conditions; no file state is an input. (c) Section 8.6 shows four conflict choices (Compare, Reload, Overwrite, Save As); src/web/editor/src/index.ts builds only Reload and Overwrite buttons, and the C++ bridge in src/editor/EditorController.h has no diff-view method and no write-to-another-path method. (d) EditorController::checkRecovery() in src/editor/EditorController.cpp emits recoveryAvailable when it finds a server-side snapshot that differs from the file on disk, but a repository-wide search finds no consumer of that signal outside the unit test, so the recovered text is discarded and the user is never asked.

**What was done.** In docs/SPEC.md: marked the three unimplemented row fields in section 4.2 as not yet implemented and added an implementation-status note naming SessionsModel::Roles; added an implementation-status note under section 8.2 naming aggregateRowState() and its five inputs; added an implementation-status note under section 8.6 naming the two buttons that exist and the two bridge methods that would be needed, while stating that the mandatory no-silent-overwrite rule is still met. No requirement was deleted. In docs/PLAN.md: added three bullets to the existing "Remaining known gaps - honest list" for (a), (b) and (c), each naming the file and the design step required, and expanded the pre-existing crash-recovery bullet to spell out that the recovered content is dropped and the unsaved work the snapshot exists to protect is silently lost.

**Why it matters.** Documentation only; no behaviour changed. The four gaps are now discoverable from both the specification and the plan instead of only by reading the code.


#### X34 — The documented keyboard shortcuts were not the implemented ones, and the real bindings were undocumented

- **Kind:** Documentation error
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `docs/SPEC.md`, `README.md`

**What was wrong.** Section 15 of docs/SPEC.md listed eight "suggested default" shortcuts, of which only one is implemented, and the shortcuts that ARE implemented appeared nowhere in the documentation - even though the empty-state text of a viewer pane tells the user to press Ctrl+Shift+P.

**Why it was wrong.** The list was aspirational and never revisited. Reading src/qml/: CommandPalette.qml declares activationSequence "Ctrl+Shift+P" (with a comment noting Qt maps Ctrl in a key sequence to Command on macOS, so it reads as Command+Shift+P there). Main.qml's paletteCommands list carries a shortcut on exactly two entries - "Ctrl+Shift+O" for Connect to Server and "Ctrl+R" for Refresh Workspace - which CommandPalette.qml turns into window-wide Shortcut objects. The other nine commands (four split commands, two close-pane commands, kill terminal, disconnect, mark agent output seen) have no key sequence. Separately, src/web/editor/src/index.ts registers Ctrl/Cmd+S on the Monaco instance to save. There is no session switcher and no next-pane command, so Ctrl+P, Ctrl+Tab and Alt+1..9 have nothing to bind to; Ctrl+K is not bound at all.

**What was done.** Rewrote section 15 of docs/SPEC.md as an "Implemented bindings" block listing the four real bindings, a paragraph explaining where each is registered and that Ctrl+S is editor-pane-scoped rather than window-wide, a note that every other command is palette-only, and a reconciliation table giving the status of each of the eight originally suggested defaults. Added a "Keyboard shortcuts" section to README.md with the same four bindings in a table and a pointer to the specification for the reasoning. I did not edit any QML.

**Why it matters.** Documentation only. A user previously had no way to learn Ctrl+Shift+O or Ctrl+R, and would have tried Ctrl+K and Ctrl+P from the specification and found them dead.


#### X35 — The recovery-snapshot location in the specification did not match the implementation

- **Kind:** Documentation error
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `docs/SPEC.md`

**What was wrong.** Section 11.3 of docs/SPEC.md said crash-recovery snapshots of unsaved work live at ~/.local/share/codeharbor/recovery/<client-id>/<pane-id>.json and use mode 0600. Neither the path nor the mode matches what the code does.

**Why it was wrong.** EditorController::recoveryPathFor() in src/editor/EditorController.cpp splits the edited file's path at the last slash and returns <directory>/.codeharbor-recovery/<filename> - so /repo/note.txt is snapshotted to /repo/.codeharbor-recovery/note.txt. The snapshot is written with the ordinary remote writeFile method, which is the only way it can be, because the frozen remote method catalog contains no dedicated recovery calls. As for the mode: writeFile in remote/src/files.ts creates a missing parent directory with mode 0o700, and creates a brand-new file with mode 0o644 masked by the process umask, so confidentiality comes from the directory rather than from the file.

**What was done.** Rewrote section 11.3 to give the real path with a worked example, then explained the choice in three bullets: the snapshot must be addressable through the ordinary file methods of section 8.3, and a path derived from the edited file needs no client identity, no pane identity and no separate index; being a sibling of the file it travels with the project and is visible where the affected file is; and it is guarded by the same revision tokens as a normal save, which is what stops two clients destroying each other's snapshots. Replaced the 0600 claim with an accurate description: the .codeharbor-recovery directory is created 0700 so its contents are unreadable by other users regardless of the individual files' modes, and a new snapshot file gets the service's normal 0644-masked-by-umask new-file mode because it goes through the same generic writeFile as project files.

**Why it matters.** Documentation only. The previous text would have sent anyone debugging lost unsaved work to a directory that is never written, and asserted a file mode the service does not set.


#### X36 — The two browser-bundle packages declared no Node version

- **Kind:** Inconsistency
- **Outcome:** Fixed
- **Files:** `src/web/terminal/package.json`, `src/web/editor/package.json`

**What was wrong.** package.json and remote/package.json both declare "engines": { "node": ">=23.6" }; the two browser-bundle workspaces src/web/terminal and src/web/editor declared no engine constraint, even though one Node version builds the whole repository.

**Why it was wrong.** The constraint was added to the root and the remote service but not propagated to the two web workspaces when they were created.

**What was done.** Added the identical "engines": { "node": ">=23.6" } block to src/web/terminal/package.json and src/web/editor/package.json. Nothing else in either file was touched. Both files, plus the two that already had the field, were re-parsed with node -e JSON.parse to confirm they are still valid JSON.

**Why it matters.** npm now warns when the bundles are built with an unsupported Node, matching the behaviour of the other two packages. No build or dependency change.


*Closing note:* All eight routed items were verified against the code before editing, and all eight were accurate as reported - none needed declining. No code file was modified: every edit is confined to docs/SPEC.md, docs/PLAN.md, docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, README.md, src/web/terminal/package.json and src/web/editor/package.json. The root package.json and remote/package.json already carried the engines field and were left untouched.

Two places where I deliberately narrowed a claim rather than write something I had not verified: in section 10.3 I did not state that every framing rule is covered by a test, because remote/test/ has tests for the batch case, the notification case, the invalid-params case and the revision-mismatch code but none specifically for blank-line skipping or for a parse error; and in the section 15 reconciliation table I removed a rationale I had drafted for why Ctrl+K was dropped, because no comment in the code gives one - the table now just records that Ctrl+K is not bound.

X32's second half (scan the two files for other section citations) turned up exactly one other citation, "SPEC 11.2" in the docs/DEVELOPMENT.md dependency table, which is correct and was left alone. README.md has no numbered citations.

No build, test, lint or typecheck command was run. tests/no_scaffolding.sh only scans .cpp/.h/.qml/.ts/.mjs/.sql/.cmake/CMakeLists.txt under src and remote, so none of these edits can trip it. The only executable check I ran was JSON.parse over the four package.json files.

## 6. Complete label index

| Label | Kind | Outcome | Title |
|---|---|---|---|
| RW1 | Inconsistency | Fixed | setLayout stored the client-supplied serverId instead of the parent session's, breaking the "a row shares its ancestors' server" rule |
| RW2 | Latent bug | Fixed | setLayout's read-then-write upsert could crash when two connections saved a layout at the same time |
| RW3 | Active bug | Fixed | Reordering groups or sessions with an incomplete list left two rows sharing one position, making their displayed order random |
| RW4 | Latent bug | Fixed | Moving a Dev Session into a group belonging to another server left the session and its panes tagged with the old server |
| RW5 | Latent bug | Fixed | Partial updates from two connections could silently erase each other's changes |
| RW6 | Footgun | Fixed | The transaction helper used a deferred BEGIN, could not be nested, and its rollback could hide the real error |
| RW7 | Latent bug | Fixed | A schema migration that failed part way left the database in a half-upgraded state |
| RW8 | Footgun | Fixed | A database written by a newer build was accepted silently instead of being refused |
| RW9 | Footgun | Fixed | Table and column names were interpolated into SQL through plain `string` parameters |
| RW10 | Simplification | Fixed | Duplicating a Dev Session re-compiled the same INSERT for every copied row, and copied each child's own server tag instead of the parent's |
| RW11 | Missing test | Fixed | No tests covered partial reorder lists, layout-row ownership, cross-server moves, the newer-database guard, or the layout table's own constraints |
| RW12 | Documentation error | Fixed | schema.sql's `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` line invited the reader to believe the file turns foreign keys on for everyone |
| RW13 | Latent bug | Not fixed (explained) | Deleting a viewer or terminal pane leaves the stored split layout pointing at the deleted pane |
| RW14 | Footgun | Not fixed (explained) | One corrupt stored layout tree makes the entire workspace listing fail |
| RW15 | Nit | Not fixed (explained) | Only one of the nineteen remote-procedure handlers validates its parameters |
| RF1 | Active bug | Fixed | Save-conflict errors named the current revision with a key the client never reads |
| RF2 | Active bug | Fixed | stat() minted a symlink's own revision while read and write follow the link, making every symlinked file look permanently conflicted |
| RF3 | Latent bug | Fixed | Temp-file cleanup on a failed save could replace the real error with a meaningless one |
| RF4 | Latent bug | Fixed | Malformed base64 upload was silently written as short, corrupt bytes and reported as a successful save |
| RF5 | Simplification | Fixed | Double decode of every file read, plus a byte-order-mark trap for the next person |
| RF6 | Simplification | Fixed | The requested read length was clamped in two places that had to stay identical |
| RF7 | Footgun | Fixed | The temporary save file was created with the target's permissions, including file-type bits |
| RF8 | Latent bug | Fixed | A tmux invocation that never returns hung the RPC method, and the client's session list, forever |
| RF9 | Documentation error | Fixed | A long security comment sat above the wrong function |
| RF10 | Inconsistency | Fixed | Inconsistency: existence checks avoid tmux's target syntax on purpose, but killing a session still uses it |
| RF11 | Footgun | Fixed | The "inside the repository" flag is a lexical hint, not a sandbox, and nothing said so |
| RF12 | Nit | Fixed | Undocumented environment variable overriding the tmux binary |
| RF13 | Missing test | Fixed | Missing tests for symlinks, byte-order marks, malformed uploads, the conflict payload, watch-before-create, and carriage returns |
| RF14 | Footgun | Not fixed (explained) | A rapidly changing watched file queues one filesystem check per change signal |
| RF15 | Latent bug | Not fixed (explained) | A whole-file read has no size ceiling |
| RF16 | Latent bug | Not fixed (explained) | The revision returned by a successful save is read back from disk after the rename |
| RR1 | Latent bug | Fixed | Command-line entry point silently does nothing when the install path needs URL escaping |
| RR2 | Latent bug | Fixed | A response that cannot be serialized killed the whole remote service |
| RR3 | Latent bug | Fixed | Writing to a closed client pipe crashed the service with EPIPE |
| RR4 | Active bug | Fixed | The service never exited after the client disconnected if any file watch was active |
| RR5 | Latent bug | Fixed | Request parameters were never type-checked, so bad input surfaced as a generic internal error |
| RR6 | Latent bug | Fixed | A handler returning nothing would put a response on the wire with neither result nor error |
| RR7 | Inconsistency | Not fixed (explained) | Batch requests (a JSON array of calls) are not supported, and nothing said so |
| RR8 | Latent bug | Fixed | A JSON array passed the "is this an object?" checks for event metadata and native events |
| RR9 | Latent bug | Fixed | The agent-status hook could block the user's agent run forever |
| RR10 | Latent bug | Fixed | The hook could exit non-zero, which a harness may read as a failed hook |
| RR11 | Footgun | Fixed | The bridge left its socket file behind when stopped by a signal, and had no shutdown path |
| RR12 | Footgun | Not fixed (explained) | Starting a second bridge silently orphans the first instead of failing |
| RR13 | Footgun | Not fixed (explained) | The server puts no upper bound on the length of one input line, while the client does |
| RR14 | Nit | Fixed | Blank-line check copied every incoming line, including multi-megabyte write requests |
| RR15 | Documentation error | Fixed | Comment claimed the file-method handlers did not exist yet |
| RR16 | Documentation error | Fixed | Hook documentation and usage text omitted the environment-variable form of the event name |
| RR17 | Missing test | Fixed | The Claude Code adapter had no tests at all |
| RR18 | Missing test | Fixed | The claim that the Pi adapter is identical to the Oh My Pi adapter was unverified |
| RR19 | Missing test | Fixed | No tests covered JSON-RPC envelope conformance |
| RR20 | Missing test | Fixed | The agent-state token list existed twice in two languages with no drift gate |
| RR21 | Missing test | Fixed | The revision-mismatch error code existed twice with no parity check |
| RR22 | Missing test | Fixed | Nothing checked that the server's schema version satisfies the client's minimum |
| RR23 | Missing test | Fixed | The hook's failure and misuse paths were untested |
| RR24 | Footgun | Not fixed (explained) | The bridge ignores back-pressure when relaying events to its output stream |
| RR25 | Nit | Not fixed (explained) | The hook's metadata field can never be populated from a real hook invocation |
| AP1 | Latent bug | Fixed | The SSH connect pre-flight waits out its whole timeout budget (and blames the wrong thing) when the probe socket fails immediately |
| AP2 | Latent bug | Fixed | Desktop notifications: a late reply can make the next repeat rewrite the WRONG bubble |
| AP3 | Latent bug | Fixed | An SSH password/passphrase the server never asked for stayed resident in the connection pool for the rest of the process |
| AP4 | Latent bug | Fixed | The 'no transport yet' guard in the sidebar reload did not actually protect against a null RPC client |
| AP5 | Latent bug | Fixed | Injecting the connection objects twice stacked duplicate signal handlers, doubling every connect error and every server identity lookup |
| AP6 | Latent bug | Fixed | Changing the server left the previous server's split layout loaded and editable, so the next pane edit could be written into the wrong server's row |
| AP7 | Simplification | Fixed | Server switch duplicated the active-session teardown logic instead of calling the function that exists for it |
| AP8 | Nit | Fixed | The credential kind was decoded before it was validated |
| AP9 | Missing test | Fixed | No in-process test covered the unknown-host-key prompt, its accept/reject answers, or the approval surviving a follow-up credential prompt |
| AP10 | Lint | Fixed | Include hygiene: an unused include and a type used without its own include |
| SH1 | Active bug | Fixed | Every finished SSH channel stayed allocated, so a session ran out of channel slots and wedged |
| SH2 | Active bug | Fixed | A byte-stream device that outlived its SSH session kept reading from freed libssh memory (use-after-free) |
| SH3 | Active bug | Fixed | Wildcard known-hosts entries were treated as literal text, so a changed key at a wildcard-covered host got the friendly 'new host' prompt instead of a hard refusal |
| SH4 | Latent bug | Fixed | Splitting a comma-separated host list would have destroyed OpenSSH's '!' exclusions, trusting the exact hosts the file carves out |
| SH5 | Latent bug | Fixed | Host names were compared case-sensitively, unlike OpenSSH, so a changed key at a differently-cased name read as first use |
| SH6 | Latent bug | Fixed | An unreadable server host key was treated as a brand-new host instead of being refused |
| SH7 | Footgun | Fixed | Passwords and key passphrases were left in freed heap memory |
| SH8 | Footgun | Fixed | Failures to apply SSH options (host, port, user, private key file) were discarded |
| SH9 | Latent bug | Fixed | Session teardown could re-enter itself and free the same session twice |
| SH10 | Inconsistency | Fixed | resizePty() sent a terminal-resize request on channels that have no terminal, contradicting its own documentation |
| SH11 | Documentation error | Fixed | Comment claimed host-key verification reuses a token that 'pinned the algorithm list' — no such pinning exists |
| SH12 | Nit | Fixed | A host-key refusal was logged with an unrelated or empty libssh error appended |
| SH13 | Latent bug | Fixed | Private key object would leak if libssh ever returned one alongside an import failure |
| SH14 | Active bug | Not fixed (explained) | Servers that offer only keyboard-interactive authentication cannot be used at all |
| SH15 | Footgun | Not fixed (explained) | The SSH handshake and channel writes run synchronously on the calling (user-interface) thread |
| SH16 | Missing test | Fixed | No tests covered wildcard/negated/differently-cased known-hosts entries or channel-slot reclamation |
| RC1 | Latent bug | Fixed | Swapping or detaching the transport silently abandoned every in-flight request callback |
| RC2 | Latent bug | Fixed | Any message containing a "method" field was treated as a server notification, even when it also carried a response id |
| RC3 | Latent bug | Fixed | Passing a null callback to call() crashed the program when the answer arrived |
| RC4 | Latent bug | Fixed | A short write left a half-written message on the wire and the client kept using the corrupted stream |
| RC5 | Latent bug | Fixed | A response that had already arrived was thrown away when the peer closed, and its caller was told the transport died |
| RC6 | Nit | Fixed | Testing whether a line was blank copied the entire multi-megabyte message |
| RC7 | Nit | Fixed | Valid JSON that was not an object produced the nonsensical warning 'malformed RPC message: no error' |
| RC8 | Documentation error | Fixed | Header documentation contradicted the guarantee the class is built around |
| RC9 | Documentation error | Fixed | RpcTypes.h claimed the cross-language drift test requires a specific declaration order, which it does not |
| RC10 | Missing test | Fixed | Nine untested contracts in the transport layer |
| RC11 | Simplification | Not fixed (explained) | Every request and result data structure in RpcTypes.h is unused code |
| RC12 | Footgun | Not fixed (explained) | The remote library publicly links two libraries it does not use, and other modules silently depend on that |
| RC13 | Latent bug | Not fixed (explained) | The remote service can send an error whose id is null, which no client can match to a request |
| RC14 | Nit | Not fixed (explained) | Two known-and-accepted inefficiencies in the read path and the id allocator, left alone on purpose |
| MP1 | Latent bug | Fixed | A corrupt saved pane layout was reported to the rest of the client as a real, valid layout containing one blank pane |
| MP2 | Latent bug | Fixed | Malformed entries inside a workspace listing became phantom groups, sessions and panes with empty identifiers |
| MP3 | Inconsistency | Fixed | The sidebar model served a display role that it did not advertise, making it unreachable from the user interface layer |
| MP4 | Lint | Fixed | Two case labels in the sidebar model were indented one level too deep |
| MP5 | Missing test | Fixed | The precedence reducer for sidebar row colour had only eight sampled test cases out of thirty-two possible inputs |
| MP6 | Missing test | Fixed | The state-name strings that cross into the browser pages and the remote service were entirely untested |
| MP7 | Missing test | Fixed | Two rejection paths in the layout-tree parser had no test coverage |
| MP8 | Missing test | Fixed | An agent in the 'starting' state counting as 'running' for the sidebar was untested |
| MP9 | Missing test | Fixed | The client's declared database schema version was documented as being kept in step with the server's, but nothing enforced it |
| MP10 | Footgun | Fixed | Nothing warned callers that a pending server-reply callback outlives the repository object that created it |
| MP11 | Documentation error | Fixed | A comment claimed the server's row timestamps live in the persistence layer, but they are discarded entirely |
| MP12 | Nit | Fixed | The priority order was documented at the wrapper but not at the function that actually implements it |
| MP13 | Nit | Not fixed (explained) | The strongly-typed identifier wrappers cannot be used as hash-map or sorted-map keys, and that is correct as it stands |
| MP14 | Nit | Not fixed (explained) | The layout tree has no mutation operations, so the whole class of node-leak, cycle and orphan bugs cannot arise |
| MP15 | Footgun | Not fixed (explained) | The sidebar model's only update path is a full reset, which is correct but coarse |
| TM1 | Latent bug | Fixed | TerminalFactory::kill() kept a hash-table iterator alive across a call that can rebuild the hash table |
| TM2 | Active bug | Fixed | A failed PTY open reported the real reason and then immediately overwrote it with a generic one |
| TM3 | Latent bug | Fixed | Re-attaching a terminal without an explicit size silently shrank the pane to 80x24 |
| TM4 | Footgun | Fixed | Two independent hand-written copies of 'which terminal states count as connected' |
| TM5 | Inconsistency | Not fixed (explained) | Three of the nine terminal lifecycle states are never produced, and the reconnect-delay helper has no caller |
| TM6 | Footgun | Fixed | The bridge's geometryChanged signal is the QML pane's attach trigger, and nothing said so |
| TM7 | Missing test | Fixed | The controller's entire transport seam and connection-drop state machine were untested |
| TM8 | Footgun | Fixed | Two test doubles used a read-write memory buffer as a terminal transport, which echoes keystrokes back as terminal output |
| TM9 | Active bug | Not fixed (explained) | Reloading the terminal web page loses everything the terminal printed during the reload |
| TM10 | Nit | Fixed | A keystroke dropped because the channel is dead is discarded with no explanation in the code |
| TM11 | Nit | Not fixed (explained) | Trimming the oldest bytes from the hidden-terminal buffer can cut a character or an escape sequence in half |
| TM12 | Latent bug | Uncertain | A remote terminal that produces no output leaves the pane stuck in 'attaching_tmux' forever |
| TM13 | Nit | Not fixed (explained) | The one-shot channel used to destroy a remote tmux session is only freed when that channel reports it finished |
| ED1 | Active bug | Fixed | Crash-recovery snapshots were never actually written on a real server (missing parent directory) |
| ED2 | Active bug | Fixed | An open file never reported the 'disconnected' state, so a pane advertised 'clean' through an outage |
| ED3 | Active bug | Fixed | A failed save left the buffer marked as clean, so the next external change silently overwrote unsaved work |
| ED4 | Active bug | Fixed | Text typed while a save was in flight was treated as saved, and its recovery copy was deleted |
| ED5 | Latent bug | Fixed | A stale file-read reply from a superseded load could overwrite the newer file's content and revision |
| ED6 | Latent bug | Fixed | A save, conflict-stat or recovery reply arriving after a file switch was applied to the new file |
| ED7 | Latent bug | Fixed | A truncated (partial) read could be written back, deleting everything past the part that was read |
| ED8 | Active bug | Fixed | The conflict payload field name did not match the server, so every conflict paid for a needless extra round trip |
| ED9 | Latent bug | Fixed | A debounced content report arriving during a file switch snapshotted the wrong file's buffer |
| ED10 | Active bug | Fixed | An editor page that connected late never learned the file state, leaving its status bar blank |
| ED11 | Inconsistency | Fixed | A conflict was silently downgraded to the weaker 'externally modified' state |
| ED12 | Documentation error | Fixed | Factory comments described 'recovery timers' that do not exist |
| ED13 | Documentation error | Fixed | refreshPermissions comment promised its continuation always runs |

| ED15 | Inconsistency | Not fixed (explained) | Recovery snapshots are stored somewhere other than the location the specification names |
| ED16 | Inconsistency | Not fixed (explained) | One state in the file lifecycle enumeration is unreachable |
| ED17 | Latent bug | Not fixed (explained) | No size limit on opening a file: a huge file is read entirely into memory |
| ED18 | Nit | Not fixed (explained) | A file deleted on the server while open leaves a stale revision baseline |

| VW2 | Active bug | Fixed | Common source-file extensions were classified as unviewable binary downloads |

| X26 | Footgun | Fixed | Editor pane created its C++ controller through an unguarded context-property lookup |
| X27 | Active bug | Fixed | Nothing showed the user that an open editor file is disconnected or in conflict |
| X28 | Inconsistency | Fixed | The four modal dialogs were drawn in the control library's default light theme |
| X29 | Documentation error | Fixed | Specification section 6.6 named agent states that do not exist |
| X30 | Documentation error | Fixed | Specification section 10.3 did not document the JSON-RPC framing rules the service enforces |

| X32 | Documentation error | Fixed | Wrong specification section cited for the two-browser-profile design |
| X33 | Documentation error | Fixed | Three specification requirements are unimplemented, plus crash recovery is never offered, and nothing recorded it |
| X34 | Documentation error | Fixed | The documented keyboard shortcuts were not the implemented ones, and the real bindings were undocumented |
| X35 | Documentation error | Fixed | The recovery-snapshot location in the specification did not match the implementation |
| X36 | Inconsistency | Fixed | The two browser-bundle packages declared no Node version |
| V1 | Lint | Fixed | New cross-language test used a raw string inside a macro, which Qt's meta-object compiler cannot parse |
| V2 | Latent bug | Fixed | Live reconnect test compared recycled memory addresses to prove a new SSH channel was created |
